<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We give a uniform algorithm that reconstructs every binary string from independent deletion traces when its length and rational retention probability are known. For each fixed retention probability, both the number of traces and the bit complexity are quasipolynomial in the string length. More generally, we give an explicit sample bound uniform over all rational retention probabilities, with running time polynomial in the sample budget and the binary input length. If the deletion probability is at most $`n^{-\varepsilon}`$ for fixed <i>ε</i> &gt; 0, the sample and running-time bounds are polynomial. Reconstruction succeeds with probability at least 2/3 for each input string.

- **claim-uniform-reconstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There exist absolute constants $C,c\ge1$ and one randomized multitape Turing machine $\mathcal A$ with the following property. For every $n\ge2$, every rational $p\in(0,1]$, and every $x\in\{0,1\}^n$, given $n,p$ in binary and access to independent ordinary deletion traces of $x$, the machine requests at most $$M_C(n,p)=\left\lceil\exp\bigl(CB(n,p)\bigr)\right\rceil$$ traces, outputs an element of $\{0,1\}^n$, and returns $x$ with probability at least $2/3$.* *For every internal random-bit sequence and every well-formed trace stream of strings of length at most $n$, it halts within $$\bigl(n+L+M_C(n,p)\bigr)^c$$ bit operations. This bound includes preprocessing, all parameter and finite-precision calculations, every received trace bit and delimiter, and the output. The only oracle operation is the external generation of the independent deletion traces.
  - Explanation: One randomized multitape Turing machine reconstructs every length-n binary string from independent deletion traces with success probability at least 2/3. The trace count is exponential in the budget B(n,p), and the bit cost is polynomial in n, L and the permitted trace count.
- **claim-elementary-moment-bounds** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-moment-bounds)): For every array satisfying <a href="#eq:moment-matrix" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:moment-matrix">[eq:moment-matrix]</a>, one has $\|m_J\|\le1$ for $\|J\|\le2D$. The value under $\mathcal E$ of any product of at most $2D$ distinct input bits has magnitude at most one. On polynomials of degree at most $D$, the form $$\langle P,R\rangle_{\mathcal E}=\mathcal E[P\overline R]$$ is a positive semidefinite Hermitian form, linear in its first argument. It satisfies Cauchy–Schwarz and becomes an inner product after quotienting by its zero-norm subspace.
  - Explanation: Positive semidefiniteness of the moment matrix forces every sign moment and every product of distinct input bits to have magnitude at most one. It also turns the functional into a positive semidefinite Hermitian form satisfying Cauchy-Schwarz.
- **claim-prefix-separation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-separation)): There exist absolute constants $C_{\mathrm{sep}}\ge1$ and an integer $H\ge1$ with the following property. For $n\ge2$ and $0<p<1$, define $$D_*=4^{\lceil\log_2 Y\rceil+H}\le4^{H+1}Y^2,$$ using <a href="#eq:aux-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:aux-parameters">[eq:aux-parameters]</a>. For every $x\in\{0,1\}^n$, every $d\in[n]$, and every positive moment functional of order $2D$ fixing $x_1,\ldots,x_{d-1},1-x_d$, where $D\ge D_*$, some nonempty $I\subseteq[n]$ with $\|I\|\le D_*$ satisfies $$\|\mathcal E[T_I]-T_I(x)\|> \exp\bigl(-C_{\mathrm{sep}}B(n,p)\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: Every positive moment functional that fixes a wrong prefix is separated from the true input by some low-order trace moment. The separation is at least exponentially small in the budget B(n,p).
- **claim-multiscale-step** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-multiscale)): There are absolute constants $0<\beta,\eta_0<1$ and $C_0,C_1\ge1$ with the following property. Let $f$ be an order-$k$ feature from <a href="#eq:feature" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:feature">[eq:feature]</a>, and let $\Delta$ arise from a moment functional of order $2D$ with the prefix conditions of Section <a href="#sec:setup" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:setup">2</a>. Suppose $$0<S\le4,\qquad 0<S'\le\min\{1,\eta_0S\},\qquad 4k\le D,$$ and, for some $\beta S\le s\le S$ and $\lvert \omega\rvert\le2.002s$, $$\label{eq:multiscale-cost} \lvert \mathcal S_f(s,\omega)\rvert\ge e^{-E},\qquad E\ge C_0\left(1+k\log(2k)+\log H_f+\log\frac1{S'}\right).$$ Then an order-$k'$ feature $f'$, with $1\le k'\le4k$, satisfies $$\label{eq:multiscale-output} \lvert \mathcal S_{f'}(s',\omega')\rvert\ge e^{-7.98E},\qquad \beta S'\le s'\le S',\qquad \lvert \omega'\rvert\le2.002s',$$ and $$\label{eq:multiscale-height} H_{f'}\le H_f^4\left(1+\frac1{\beta S'}\right)^3.$$ Moreover, there are $1\le m\le4$ lists, each equal to the old parameter list $(z_1,\ldots,z_{k-1})$ or its complex conjugate, and at most $m-1$ added parameters, with the following property. Each new inner-gap parameter is a nonempty product using at most one entry from each list and at most one added parameter. Every added parameter $e^{-a+\mathrm i\theta}$ obeys $$\label{eq:added-parameters} a\ge\beta S',\qquad \lvert \theta\rvert\le C_1S.$$
  - Explanation: A detectable signal at decay S is moved to a much smaller decay S' at the cost of at most a fourfold increase in feature order. The logarithm of the signal worsens by less than a factor of eight, and the height and the new parameters stay controlled.
- **claim-cauchy-matching** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cauchy-matching)): Let $w(v)=1/(\pi(1+v^2))$. For every $r\ge0$ there is a finite positive measure $\mu_r$ on $\mathbb R^2$, supported on $v-v'=r$, such that the sum of its two marginals is bounded above by $w(v)\,dv$. For $r>0$ its total mass is $$\label{eq:cauchy-matching-mass} 2\mu_r(\mathbb R^2)=1-\frac4\pi\arctan(e^{-\pi/r});$$ for $r=0$ its total mass is $1/2$. In particular, the quantity in <a href="#eq:cauchy-matching-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cauchy-matching-mass">[eq:cauchy-matching-mass]</a> is greater than $0.737$ when $0<r\le2$.
  - Explanation: For every difference r there is a positive measure on pairs with that difference whose marginals are dominated by the Cauchy density. Its total mass is given explicitly and is bounded below for small r.
- **claim-good-frequencies** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-good-frequencies)): Let $s_1,E>0$, let $\lvert \omega\rvert\le2.003s_1$, and let $\Lambda:\mathbb R\to[0,\infty]$ be measurable and satisfy <a href="#eq:poisson-log-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:poisson-log-bound">[eq:poisson-log-bound]</a>. There is a real $\lambda$ with $$\label{eq:single-frequency} \lvert \lambda\rvert\le2s_1,\qquad \Lambda(\lambda)\le2.40E.$$ There is also an absolute $C_2$ such that, for every $\lvert y\rvert\le2s_1$, some real $t,t'$ satisfy $$\label{eq:paired-frequencies} t-t'=y,\qquad \lvert t\rvert,\lvert t'\rvert\le C_2s_1,\qquad \Lambda(t)+\Lambda(t')\le2.74E.$$
  - Explanation: A bounded Poisson average of the logarithmic potential yields one frequency with small potential, and for each prescribed difference a pair of frequencies with small total potential.
- **claim-gram-chain** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-gram-chain)): Let $v_0,\ldots,v_J$, with $J\ge1$, lie in a complex Hilbert space whose inner product is linear in its first argument. Suppose $\lVert v_0\rVert=1$ and, for some $\xi\ge0$, $$\lvert \lVert v_i\rVert^2-1\rvert\le\xi\quad(0\le i\le J),\qquad \lvert \langle v_i,v_{i-1}\rangle-1\rvert\le\xi\quad(1\le i\le J).$$ Then $$\lvert \langle v_J,v_0\rangle-1\rvert\le2J^2\xi.$$
  - Explanation: If squared norms and neighbouring inner products along a chain of vectors are within xi of one, the endpoint inner product with the first vector is within a quadratic multiple of xi of one.
- **claim-balanced-product-signal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-balanced-product)): Some balanced product $G$ satisfies $$\label{eq:balanced-product-signal} \lvert \Delta[G]\rvert\ge e^{-7.94E}.$$
  - Explanation: Gram chaining under positivity forces at least one balanced product of windowed feature sums to keep a detectable discrepancy.
- **claim-edge-gap-assignment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-edge-gap-assignment)): Let a connected multigraph have ordered vertices $i_1<\cdots<i_v$. There is an assignment of a distinct edge to each gap $(i_a,i_{a+1})$ such that its assigned edge crosses that gap.
  - Explanation: In a connected multigraph on ordered vertices, each consecutive gap can be given its own distinct crossing edge. This prevents double charging of damping costs.
- **claim-product-cayley** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-product-cayley)): Let $N\ge1$ be an integer and $0<\gamma\le1$. Suppose $z$ is a nonempty product of at most $N$ numbers $e^{-a_j+\mathrm i\theta_j}$, where $a_j>0$, $\theta_j\in\mathbb R$, and $$a_j\ge\gamma\min\{\lvert \theta_j\rvert,N\theta_j^2\}.$$ There is an absolute $c_3>0$ such that $$\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}\frac{1+z}{1-z}\ge c_3\gamma.$$
  - Explanation: A product of boundedly many damped unit-modulus parameters keeps the Cayley image at a positive distance from the imaginary axis, proportional to the damping constant.
- **claim-prefix-slack** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-prefix-slack)): On $\mathcal G$, a prefix whose first incorrect bit is at its last position has no solution to <a href="#eq:prefix-feasibility" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:prefix-feasibility">[eq:prefix-feasibility]</a>. Every correct prefix has a moment vector $m^*\in[-1,1]^l$ such that, writing $G$ for the dimension of its moment matrix, $$\label{eq:prefix-slack} M(m^*)\succeq(\zeta/8)\operatorname{Id}_G,\qquad \|\mathcal E_{m^*}[T_I]-\widehat t_I\|\le\zeta/2 \quad(I\in\mathcal I_D).$$
  - Explanation: On the good statistical event a wrong prefix is infeasible, while every correct prefix has a moment vector with strict positive-definite and statistical slack.
- **claim-rational-feasibility** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-rational-feasibility)): Let $r\ge0$ and $D\ge1$ be integers, and let $m$ consist of the $$l=\sum_{j=1}^{\min(2D,r)}\binom rj$$ nonempty sign moments. Let $M(m)$ be their moment matrix, of dimension $$G=\sum_{j=0}^{\min(D,r)}\binom rj.$$ Suppose a finite list of rational affine inequalities $\ell_t(m)\le b_t$ is given, each with nonconstant coefficient vector of $\ell^1$-norm at most $1$, together with a dyadic $0<\zeta\le1/4$.* *There is a deterministic rational procedure which either returns a vector satisfying $$m\in[-1,1]^l,\qquad M(m)\succeq0,\qquad \ell_t(m)\le b_t \quad\text{for all }t,$$ or reports failure. It returns a vector whenever some $m^*\in[-1,1]^l$ satisfies $$M(m^*)\succeq(\zeta/8)\operatorname{Id}_G,\qquad \ell_t(m^*)\le b_t-\zeta/2\quad\text{for all }t.$$ Put $\lambda=\zeta/(16G)$. The procedure performs at most $\lceil10(1+l)/\lambda^2\rceil$ feasibility checks, each taking a number of bit operations polynomial in the list dimensions, the input bit lengths, and $\log(1/\zeta)$. These time bounds hold without the slack assumption.
  - Explanation: A deterministic rational relaxation procedure returns an exactly feasible moment vector whenever a slack solution exists, with a capped number of feasibility checks and polynomial cost per check.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cauchy-matching",
        "exact_quote": "Let $w(v)=1/(\\pi(1+v^2))$. For every $r\\ge0$ there is a finite positive measure $\\mu_r$ on $\\mathbb R^2$, supported on $v-v'=r$, such that the sum of its two marginals is bounded above by $w(v)\\,dv$. For $r>0$ its total mass is $$\\label{eq:cauchy-matching-mass}\n 2\\mu_r(\\mathbb R^2)=1-\\frac4\\pi\\arctan(e^{-\\pi/r});$$ for $r=0$ its total mass is $1/2$. In particular, the quantity in <a href=\"#eq:cauchy-matching-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cauchy-matching-mass\">[eq:cauchy-matching-mass]</a> is greater than $0.737$ when $0<r\\le2$.",
        "source_id": "PM-122.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cauchy-matching",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **trace observable** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For a nonempty rank set $I=\{\ell_1<\cdots<\ell_k\}\subseteq[n]$, the corresponding trace observable is the product of the bits at those ranks, with value zero if the trace has length less than $\ell_k$.
- **positive moment functional** ([source](#pm-h-8)): We call it a *positive moment functional of order $2D$ fixing $u$*.
- **discrepancy functional** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For a positive moment functional fixing $u$, write $$\Delta[P]=\mathcal E[P]-P(x).$$
- **feature height** ([source](#pm-h-9)): Put $$H_f=\prod_{h=1}^{k-1}(1+1/a_h).$$
- **balanced product** ([source](#pm-h-13)): A product of one to four factors, each of the form $A(t)$ or $\overline{A(t)}$ with $\lvert t\rvert\le C_3S$, is called *balanced* if the sum of its signed frequencies has magnitude at most $b$; conjugation changes the sign of a frequency.
- **interleaving** ([source](#eq:endpoint-window)): An *interleaving* specifies the ordering, including all ties, of the $mk$ labeled positions in these tuples.

| q-trace-count | number of requested traces M_C(n,p) | ≤ | $\left\lceil\exp\bigl(CB(n,p)\bigr)\right\rceil$ | n at least 2, p rational in (0,1] |
| q-bit-operations | bit operations before halting | ≤ | $\bigl(n+L+M_C(n,p)\bigr)^c$ | n at least 2, rational p |
| q-success-probability | success probability | ≥ | $2/3$ | every x in {0,1}^n |
| q-separation-degree | cutoff degree D_* | ≤ | $4^{H+1}Y^2$ | n at least 2, 0<p<1 |
| q-separation-gap | trace moment separation | > | $\exp\bigl(-C_{\mathrm{sep}}B(n,p)\bigr)$ | D at least D_*, 0<p<1 |
| q-multiscale-signal | signal after one multiscale step | ≥ | $e^{-7.98E}$ | hypotheses of the proposition |
| q-cauchy-mass | twice the total mass of the matching measure | > | $0.737$ | 0<r at most 2 |
| q-gram-error | endpoint inner product deviation | ≤ | $2J^2\xi$ | J at least 1 |
| q-balanced-signal | discrepancy of a balanced product | ≥ | $e^{-7.94E}$ | some balanced product G |
| q-trace-sample-size | decoder trace count T | = | $512(1+N)\zeta^{-2}$ | 0<p<1, dyadic tolerance |
| q-failure-probability | probability of the bad statistical event | ≤ | $\frac1{32}$ | each fixed input x |
| q-feasibility-step | relaxation step size lambda | = | $\zeta/(16G)$ | dyadic zeta at most 1/4 |
| q-feasibility-checks | number of feasibility checks | ≤ | $\lceil10(1+l)/\lambda^2\rceil$ | all rational input lists, with or without slack |
| q-decoder-success | probability the decoder outputs x | > | $2/3$ | each fixed x |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-moment-bounds** [claim](#stmt-lem-moment-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-moment-bounds): Principal two-by-two submatrix argument, then Gram-form reading of the positive semidefinite matrix. -> Bounded moments and a positive semidefinite Hermitian form with Cauchy-Schwarz.
- **step-gram-chain** [claim](#stmt-lem-gram-chain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-gram-chain): Telescoping of increments with Cauchy-Schwarz estimates. -> Quadratic endpoint error bound.
- **step-cauchy-matching** [claim](#stmt-lem-cauchy-matching) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cauchy-matching): Alternating-sum edge weights along chains, nonnegativity from a Fourier series, mass computed as an arctangent series. -> Pairing measure with explicit mass.
- **step-good-frequencies** [claim](#stmt-lem-good-frequencies) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-good-frequencies): Averaging against the Cauchy measure, with the matching measure for pairs. -> Good single and paired frequencies.
- **step-balanced-product** [claim](#stmt-lem-balanced-product) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-balanced-product): Proof by contradiction building a normalized chain of polynomial vectors and applying Gram chaining. -> A balanced product with retained signal.
- **step-edge-gap** [claim](#stmt-lem-edge-gap-assignment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-edge-gap-assignment): Invertible crossing matrix of a spanning tree and a nonzero determinant term. -> Distinct edge per gap.
- **step-multiscale** [claim](#stmt-prop-multiscale) / [proof](#subsec:interleavings): Gaussian window, balanced product, Gaussian contour deformation, interleaving expansion and height control. -> Feature at smaller decay with retained signal.
- **step-product-cayley** [claim](#stmt-lem-product-cayley) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-product-cayley): Summing damping hypotheses and estimating modulus and distance to one. -> Cayley margin proportional to the damping constant.
- **step-separation** [claim](#stmt-thm-separation) / [proof](#pm-h-23): Iterate the multiscale step, apply the trace generating identity, then continue analytically across a single strip. -> Quantitative separation of trace moments.
- **step-prefix-slack** [claim](#stmt-lem-prefix-slack) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-prefix-slack): Contradiction with separation for wrong prefixes; mixture with uniform signs for correct prefixes. -> Infeasible wrong prefix and slack for correct prefix.
- **step-rational-feasibility** [claim](#stmt-lem-rational-feasibility) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rational-feasibility): Projected relaxation with exact Schur-complement matrix check and rounding to a dyadic grid. -> Exactly feasible moment vector or failure within a fixed cap.
- **step-main-decoder** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rational-feasibility-2): Choose integer parameters, collect traces, test both extensions of each prefix by rational feasibility, and bound resources. -> Uniform reconstruction with the stated sample and bit bounds.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-coefficient-mass**: For a fixed rank set, the coefficients of the trace-moment polynomial are nonnegative, they sum to at most one, and they are rational when p is rational.
- **inv-explicit-coefficient**: The coefficient of a bit monomial in the trace-moment polynomial is a product of binomial counts for the prefix and the open gaps, times the retention factor for the selected positions and the deletion factor for the remaining positions up to the last selected one.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-non-deletion-channel**: Apply the explicit coefficient formula to a channel that also inserts or flips bits, or whose retention probability varies by position. -> The formula multiplies independent binomial probabilities, so with other error types the computed coefficients no longer equal the trace probabilities.
- **anti-read-coefficients-from-data**: Treat the coefficients as something a decoder can read off from the observed traces. -> The coefficients depend on input positions that traces do not reveal, so they must be computed from the model, not measured.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The full decoder runs on a trace budget exponential in the sample exponent and cannot be executed exhaustively. The explicit coefficient formula for the trace-moment polynomial is a finite exact computation, so Panel 3 checks that formula against direct enumeration of all deletion masks.

```python
# INPUTS: stdin tokens n a b k l_1 ... l_k (length, retention p=a/b, rank set of size k with l_1<...<l_k<=n).
# PRECONDITIONS: n>=1, 0<a<=b, 1<=k<=n, strictly increasing ranks, all at most n.
# OUTPUTS: one line per input tuple with nonzero coefficient: 'i1,...,ik num/den' in lexicographic order.
# TIME: O(C(n,k)*k) big-integer operations (tuple enumeration).
# SPACE: O(C(n,k)) output lines.
# ARITHMETIC: exact Fractions and integer binomials, no floating point.
# FAILURE: malformed input raises an exception and exits with a nonzero status.
import sys
from fractions import Fraction
from itertools import combinations
from math import comb


def c(n, r):
    return comb(n, r) if 0 <= r <= n else 0


def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    n, a, b, k = int(data[0]), int(data[1]), int(data[2]), int(data[3])
    ls = [int(t) for t in data[4:4 + k]]
    p = Fraction(a, b)
    q = 1 - p
    out = []
    for t in combinations(range(1, n + 1), k):
        w = c(t[0] - 1, ls[0] - 1)
        for j in range(k - 1):
            w *= c(t[j + 1] - t[j] - 1, ls[j + 1] - ls[j] - 1)
        if w == 0:
            continue
        val = w * p ** ls[-1] * q ** (t[-1] - ls[-1])
        if val != 0:
            out.append(','.join(str(x) for x in t) + ' ' + str(val.numerator) + '/' + str(val.denominator))
    print('\n'.join(out))


main()
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves an existence statement about a machine with unspecified absolute constants and a quasipolynomial sample budget. It gives no reusable library interface, so a software artefact would have to be invented rather than read off. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No simulation or game mechanic is described. Sampling deletion traces would only serve to test the decoder, and the paper treats trace generation as an external oracle. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper gives a proof-oriented decoding rule, not a command-line or analysis tool. The bit-by-bit prefix test only describes the machine inside the proof. |
| algorithms | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | Theorem 1 describes an explicit randomized decoder: collect empirical rank statistics from independent traces, then extend a prefix one bit at a time using exact feasibility tests. The length and rational retention probability are known inputs. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No new data structure is introduced. The decoder only enumerates rank tuples and bit monomials, and the paper stresses that complete trace masks are never stored. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Exact rational arithmetic on a dyadic grid is mentioned, but no circuit, parallel scheme or hardware cost model is given. The statement that iterates stay on one grid does not amount to an accelerator design. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The model is a random i.i.d. deletion channel with a worst-case unknown string. There are no adversaries, keys or security definitions, so nothing here yields a cryptographic guarantee. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The task is exact combinatorial reconstruction of one binary string with a proven success probability, not fitting a model to data. The decoder uses exact positivity tests rather than gradient training. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Lemma 12 gives a deterministic projected relaxation for affine inequalities plus a positive semidefinite moment-matrix constraint over rationals. It succeeds whenever a slack solution exists and every reported vector satisfies the exact inequalities. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A single machine reads independent traces. No replication, consensus, failure model or communication protocol is analysed, and traces carry no origin information to coordinate nodes. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The low-deletion regime is already covered by the main theorem, and no separate engineering field is supported beyond the eleven fixed domains. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-known-length-and-rational-p** (hypothesis): The machine needs the original length and the retention probability as exact binary inputs, with the probability a rational in lowest terms. The guarantee says nothing for an unknown length or an unknown or irrational retention probability. Blocked use: ['Running the decoder when the string length is unknown', 'Plugging in an estimated or irrational retention probability and still quoting the success bound']
- **limit-pure-deletion-channel** (hypothesis): The trace model is an independent deletion channel with one common retention probability. The coefficient formulas and the generating identity are derived for exactly this channel, so they do not transfer to channels with insertions, substitutions, position-dependent retention or context-dependent errors. Blocked use: ['Applying the decoder to reads with substitutions or insertions', 'Using the guarantee for sequencing reads whose deletions depend on context']
- **limit-unspecified-constants** (formal_scope): Theorem 1 asserts the existence of absolute constants and of a machine. The constants are not given numerically, so the statement yields no concrete trace count or running time for a given n and p. Blocked use: ['Quoting a concrete number of traces for a deployment', 'Claiming a measured running time follows from the theorem']
- **limit-success-probability-only** (edge_case): Correctness holds only with probability at least two thirds for each fixed input string. A single run can output a wrong string, and the guarantee is per input, not simultaneous over all inputs. Blocked use: ['Treating one run as a certified reconstruction', 'Using the output without repetition or verification where errors are costly']
- **limit-quasipolynomial-regime** (approximation): The sample and time bounds are exponential in the budget, which is only quasipolynomial for fixed retention probability. They are polynomial only when the deletion probability is at most a negative power of n. For retention probability shrinking with n the bound can be far worse. Blocked use: ['Expecting polynomial cost for a fixed constant deletion probability', 'Assuming small cost when the retention probability is close to zero']
- **limit-separation-prob-below-one** (edge_case): The separation theorem is stated for retention probability strictly between zero and one and for relaxation degree at least the cutoff. The case of retention probability one is not covered by it and is handled by a separate trivial branch. Blocked use: ['Invoking the separation bound at retention probability one', 'Using a relaxation degree below the cutoff and keeping the separation conclusion']
- **limit-slack-needed-for-success** (hypothesis): The rational feasibility procedure is guaranteed to return a vector only when a solution with strict slack exists. Without slack it may report failure even for a feasible system, so failure is not a proof of infeasibility. Blocked use: ['Reading a reported failure as proof that no moment system exists', 'Dropping the slack margin and keeping the guaranteed termination with success']
- **limit-nonconstructive-analytic-witnesses** (nonconstructive): The separation proof rests on analytic witnesses whose existence is shown but which the algorithm never computes. Nothing in the paper turns these witnesses into an algorithmic procedure, and the decoder does not need them. Blocked use: ['Extracting explicit witnesses or constants from the proof by running the algorithm', 'Treating the analytic part as a constructive procedure']
- **limit-nonlocal-statistics** (formal_scope): The statistics used involve a few trace ranks that can lie anywhere in the trace. The comparison with known barriers depends on this nonlocal feature, so restricting to short consecutive blocks of the trace breaks the argument. Blocked use: ['Implementing the test with only short sliding windows of a trace', 'Citing the result as an upper bound for local statistical query models']
